36<term> lotus clan, lotus family37<term> lotus clan, lotus family (Skt: abjakula, padmakula): \n1) One of the five buddha families. This family is associated mainly with the bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara and includes deities such as Tārā and Bhṛkuṭī. This family is presided over by the Tathāgata Amitābha.\n2) One of the five buddha families.\n3) One of the three, four, or five clans into which esoteric Buddhist deities are organized. In Kriyātantra literature, the head of this clan is Avalokiteśvara.\n4) This family is associated mainly with the bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, and includes deities such as Tārā, Bhṛkuṭī, and so forth. In the higher tantras, this family is presided over by the tathāgata Amitābha.\n\n(Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-8619.html)39<term> abjakula, padmakula43• pā. padmakulam, kulabhedaḥ — {ral gri'i rigs dang rin po che'i rigs dang pad+ma'i rigs dang 'khor lo'i rigs dang gri gug gi rigs la sngags rnams bsgrubs nas} khaḍgakule ratnakule padmakule cakrakule kartikākule mantrān sādhayitvā vi.pra.151kha/3.97; kamalakulam — {lus kyi 'khor lo drug la khams drug gi dbang gis rigs drug tu 'gyur te}…{spyi bor chu'i pad+ma la pad+ma'i rigs su 'gyur ro//} śarīre ṣaḍdhātuvaśena ṣaṭcakreṣu ṣaṭkulāni bhavanti…śirasi toyakamale kamalakulaṃ bhavati vi.pra.231kha/2.28; abjakulam — {rig pa'i rgyal po pad+ma'i rigs kyang 'di lta ste} abjakule ca vidyārājāḥ \n tadyathā ma.mū.95kha/7; \n\n• vi. padmakulī — g.{yung mo rdo rje'i rigs su bshad/} /{gar ma de bzhin pad ma'i rigs//} ḍombī vajrakulī khyātā naṭī padmakulī tathā \n he.ta.19ka/60.51{bya rgyud rigs drug gi nang gses/ mtshan gzhi pad+ma'i rigs kyi gtso bo spyan ras gzigs lta bu/}55{rigs gsum gyi nang gses shig}

9{pan chen blo bzang chos kyi rgyal mtshan} 1st Panchen Lama
51{paN chen sku phreng dang po blo bzang chos kyi rgyal mtshan gyi gsung 'bum ni/( ka par )blo bzang chos kyi rgyal mtshan gyi rnam thar spyod tshul gsal bar ston pa nor bu'i 'phreng ba/ 'khrungs rab 54{khong ni rab byung bcu gcig pa'i chu mo yos lo ste spyi lo} 1663 {lor gtsang thob rgyal gyi yul du yab brul tshang pa'i gdung rgyud zhabs drung bsod nams dbang grags dang /} {yum tshe brtan bu 9{pan chen blo bzang ye shes dpal bzang po} 2nd Panchen Lama [d 1737
51{paN chen sku phreng gnyis pa blo bzang ye shes kyi gsung 'bum ni/ ( ka par )sku gzhon nu nyid na sangs rgyas bstan pa'i sgor zhugs tshul/ mtshan nyid dang ldan pa'i bla ma bsten tshul/ mdo rgyud gzhung lugs la thos bsam mdzad tshul/ thos bsam sgom gsum dang 'chad rtsod rtsom sogs kyis bstan pa la bya ba byas tshul/ sku gsung thugs rten bzhengs pa dang mchod pa dang / ris med pa'i dge 'dun la bsnyen bkur ba la sogs pa'i rnam thar 'od dkar can gyi 'phreng ba la bzhi brgya tham pa/ ( kha par ) snyan ngag me long ma le'u gnyis pa'i gzugs can bcu gnyis yan gyi dper brjod mtsho byung rgyas pa'i me tog mdo rgyud zab mo'i lung rjes gnang dbang khrid thob pa'i gsan yig gzhan phan rin chen dbang rgyal/ glegs bam dang po gnyis pa gnyis lung med/ ( ga par )byang chub lam rim gyi bla ma brgyud pa rnams kyi rnam par thar pa pad+ma dkar po'i 'phreng ba la/ byang chub lam rim dmar khrid myur lam/ lam gyi gtso bo rnam gsum gyi rnam bshad gsung rab kun gyi gnad bsdus pa legs bshad snying po/ snying po don gsum gyi khrid yig thams cad mkhyen par bgrod pa'i nye lam/ paN chen blo bzang chos kyi rgyal mtshan gyi rnam thar nor bu'i 'phreng ba'i smad cha/ rje drung sprul sku dge 'dun chos rgyal dbang phyug gi rnam thar grub dbang dgyes pa'i rol mtsho/ bla ma'i rnal 'byor gyi 'grel pa bka' drin gsol 'debs/ blo chen rgyal kun ma/ rgyud 'dus pa'i bshad pa rdzogs pa'i 'og tu rdo rje slob dpon la gtang rag gi mchod pa bya tshul/ spyan ras gzigs rgyal ba rgya mtsho'i bla brgyud gsol 'debs shis brjod dang bcas pa/ spyan ras gzigs rgyal ba rgya mtsho'i lha dgu'i sgrub thabs/ de yi dkyil 'khor gyi cho ga de yi sbyin sreg dris lan yi ger bkod pa 'ga' zhig phyogs gcig tu bsdebs pa/ tshe sgrub 'chi med bdud rtsi'i chu rgyun/ 'phags ma gdugs dkar po can gyi sgrub thabs mchog thun dngos grub char 'bebs/ gshin po rjes 'dzin thar pa'i lam ston/ mgon dkar yid bzhin nor bu'i gtor chog dge legs kun 'byung / ye shes mgon po gri gug tsher gseb chos skyong / bstan ma gsum gyi gtor chog ( nga par )bla ma'i rnal 'byor/ bla ma'i bstod tshogs/ gsol 'debs/ yi dam 'ga' la brten pa'i bla ma'i rnal 'byor/ brtan bzhugs tshigs bcad ma/ lha 'ga'i sgrub thabs dang rjes gnang bya tshul/ bsnyen gnas srung ba dang / smyung gnas byas pa'i phan yon/ sku gdung dkar chag le tshan/ sku gsung thugs rten bzhengs pa'i rgya ba yig dgon sde 'ga' zhig gi khrims su bca' ba/ rten gsum bzhengs pa sogs kyis mtshon dge rtsa byang chub kyi rgyur bsngo ba'i smon lam tshigs bcad/ bstan srung rgya mtshor mchod gtor 'bul tshul/ 'phrin bcol/ bsangs kyi cho ga rnams so/ /paN chen blo bzang ye shes kyi gsung 'bum glegs bam bzhi rdzogs so/ /}
54{khong gi 'khrungs lo dang 'das lo mi gsal/} {shangs byed mdo'i zhal ngor sku 'khrungs/} {kun mkhyen legs pa don grub kyis mdo sngags kyi chos ma lus pa bum pa gang byo'i tshul du bstsal/} {dpal 'khor chos sder rab 'byams grwa skor mdzad pas mkhas pa'i snyan grags che/} {rgyang ro lha khang gi bla ma'ang yun ring du mdzad/} {kun mkhyen legs pa don grub kyi bka' bzhin shar rtse grwa tshang gi mkhan po lo shas mdzad/} {kun mkhyen pa'i yig cha dang mi mthun pa gzhan 'chang sa med pa'i srol btsugs/} {khong ni bkra shis lhun po'i khri pa dgu pa yin/} {khong gi gsung rtsam ni kun mkhyen dam pa'i yig cha'i }[969]{zur 'debs/} {'dul ba'i spyi don rin chen phreng ba gsal bar byed pa'i sgron me/} {mdzod kyi rnam bshad mkhas pa'i yid 'phrog} {dbu ma'i spyi don dgongs pa rab rgyan/} {phar phyin spyi don dgongs pa gsal ba'i me long /} {kun mkhyen chen po'i dgongs pa bzhin po ti bzhi'i gzhung 'grel/} {de min sngags phyogs kyi rim gnyis dang /} {rgyud 'grel sogs kyi legs bshad mang du bzhugs so/ /}
0Paṇ-chen Sö-nam-drag-pa; [1478-1554]; textbook author for the Lo-sel-ling College of Dre-pung University

93rd Panchen Lama [d 1779. {pan chen dpal ldan ye shes} 3rd Panchen Lama
51{bkra shis lhun po'i paN chen sku phreng gsum pa dpal ldan ye shes kyi gsung 'bum ni/ ( kha )brtan bzhugs gsol 'debs la shog bu zhe gcig /myur byon la bdun/ rgyal ba 'jam dpal rgya mtsho'i brtan la bzhi bcu/ bshags sdom la bdun/ chab shog la bcu/ gnas bstod la gsum/ smon shis la lnga/ bde lam myong khrid la so bdun/ bde mchog lus dkyil cho ga la bcu dgu/ gsang 'dus 'jam rdor bdag mdun bum gsum sta gon bcas la sum cu/ rten la na bza' 'dod gsol la bco lnga/ khyon bzhi brgya dang gsum/ ( ga )sku gong ma paN chen blo bzang ye shes kyi rnam thar mjug la brgya dang so brgyad/ rje byams pa'i rnam thar la brgya dang nga gsum/ gnas brtan bcu drug rnam thar la dgu/ rgyal ba bskal bzang rgya mtsho'i sku gdung dkar chag la don dgu/ yongs 'dzin blo bzang bzod pa'i rnam thar la nyer gnyis/ khyon bzhi brgya dang gnyis/( nga )'dod lha sna tshogs rang gi bla ma dbyer med rnal 'byor bdun cu skor la brgya dang zhe dgu/ lU hi pa'i cho ga bsdus pa la bcu gnyis/ bde mchog dang phyag rdor 'khor chen dang / lha brgya ma dang / dus 'khor bcas kyi bsnyen yig la bcu gcig /'pho ba la drug /sgrol ma maN+Dal bzhi pa'i cho ga sogs sgrub thabs bzhi bcu skor la brgya dang drug /ma Ni ril sgrub la bdun/ rten bzhengs tshul la bdun/ lha sna tshogs la sbyar chog sbyin sreg dang / 'jigs byed dang / phyag drug pa dang / lcam sring dang / sgrol ma dang / 'jam dbyangs dang dus 'khor bcas kyi sbyin sreg la so gnyis/ tshe dbang la bcu drug /bzang spyod 'chi bslu la brgyad/ lha tshogs gtor 'bul la so drug gsang 'dus tshogs mchod la bzhi/ bka' sogs la drug /khyon bzhi brgya dang gsum/ ( ca )lha mo mgon 36<person> Pāṇḍava37<person> Pāṇḍava (Skt: pāṇḍava): Five brothers who were the sons of Paṇḍu. The most famous was Arjuna (of Bhagavadgīta fame); the other four were Yudhiṣṭhira, Nakula, Sahadeva, and Bhīmasena. The story of the Pāṇḍava brothers and their battle with their cousins, the Kauravas, is the subject of the Mahābhārata, India's greatest epic. In the sūtra, Bali imprisons the Pāṇḍavas and Kauravas together. (Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-34049.html)\n\nSynonym: {skya bseng}38Synonym: {skya bseng}39<person> pāṇḍava

9kotita23kolitaḥ36<person> Kaulita, Kolita37<person> Kaulita, Kolita (Skt: kaulita, kolita): \n1) Another name of Maudgalyāyana. The meaning of the Tibetan is “Born from the Lap.”\n2) The name given to Maudgalyāyana by his relatives because it seemed to them he had come to them from the lap of the gods.\n3) Another name of Mahāmaudgalyāyana.\n4) Foremost in terms of miraculous abilities among the followers of the buddha Śākyamuni.\n\n(Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-3103.html)\n\nSynonym: {'dza' man}38Synonym: {'dza' man}39<person> kaulita, kolita43nā. kolitaḥ, parivrājakaḥ/sthaviraḥ — {pang nas skyes dang nye rgyal te/} /{kun tu rgyu ni gnyis po sngon/} /{dge slong dngos por rab rdzogs pas/} /{zhi ba dag tu zhugs par byas//} kolitaṃ copatiṣyaṃ ca dvau parivrājakau purā \n prapannau bhikṣubhāvena cakāra śamasaṃvṛtau \n\n a.ka.121kha/18.3; {pang nas skyes kyi le'u ste gsum pa'o//} kolitavargastṛtīyaḥ vi.va.287kha/1.107; (= {tshe dang ldan pa maud gal gyi bu chen po} āyuṣmān mahāmaudgalyāyanaḥ) \n51<{ko li taH}> {mo'u gal gyi bu mu stegs pa yin pa'i skabs kyi ming yin/ 'di dang nye rgyal } ({shA ri'i bu'i thog ma'i ming }) {gnyis 'phags pa rta thul gyi slob ma yin/ 'di dang shA ri'i bu'i lo rgyus 'dul ba lung gzhi glegs bam ( ka par )par bzhugs so/ /khye'u 'di dbang phyug sogs lha bsten nas skyes pas/ pang nas skyes zhes btags shing / maud gal gyi rigs yin pas maud gal bu zhes btags par gong gi 'dul ba lung gzhi na gsal lo/ /}52(Skt. kolitaḥ) whence born

9when a child is born 'byung ba'i ma'i tshad nad cig10SRZT 124.26{phru gu skye skabs su 'byung ba'i ma'i tshad nad cig}43mocaḥ — {btung ba brgyad po tso tsa'i btung ba dang chu shing gi 'bras bu'i} ({pang tshad kyi} (lhā.saṃ.356ka){btung ba dang rgya shug gi btung ba dang ashwat+tha'i btung ba dang u dum ba ra'i btung ba dang rtsub 'gyur gyi btung ba dang 'bra go'i btung ba dang rgun gyi btung ba khyer te} aṣṭau pānānyādāya cocapānaṃ mocapānaṃ kolapānamaśvatthapānamudumbarapānaṃ paruṣikapānaṃ kharjūrapānaṃ mṛdvīkāpānaṃ ca vi.va.38kha/1.156.55{phru gu skye skabs su 'byung ba'i ma'i tshad nad cig}
45Sanskrit: Sajjana <Personal Name>\n\n\nTibetan renderings of Sajjana: {sad dza na/}, {bram ze sad dza na/}, {paṇḍi ta mkhas pa chen po sad dza na chen po/}\n\nA Kashmiri paṇḍita who contributed to transmitting Maitreyaʼs Five Treatises to Tibet. He is the grandson of Ratnavajra, and is the father of Mahājana. His disciples include rNgog Blo ldan shes rab (1059–1109), bTsan Kha bo che (b. 1021), and gZu dGaʼ baʼi rdo rje. He aided Pa tshab nyi ma grags (b. 1055?) in translating Candrakīrti’s Catuḥśatakaṭīkā. His grandfather Ratnavajra was one of the six gatekeepers (mkhas pa sgo drug) of Vikramaśīla monastery, on which one of the earliest sources is the Chos ’byung me tog snying po’i sbrang rtsi’i bcud of Nyang ral Nyi ma’i ’od zer (ca. 1124–1192), and the next earliest is probably the Zhib mo rdo rje by dMar ston Chos kyi rgyal po (ca. 1198–1259). According to Tāranātha, Ratnavajra was originally from Kashmir, and after his long sojourn in east India, he returned to Kashmir.\nSajjana collaborated in  the Tibetan translations of the Ratnagotravibhāga and its verse version (P 5525, 5526; D 4024, 4025) and in the revision of  the Tibetan translation of the Mahāyānasūtrālaṃkāra (P 5521, D 4020).\n\nSee the full entry at:https://www.itlr.net/hwid:94178
